Adult-Onset Myasthenia Gravis

Autoimmune MONDO:0018324 Pathograph 56 Show in embeddings browser Myasthenia Gravis Neuromuscular Disease Autoimmune Disease

Adult-onset myasthenia gravis is acquired autoimmune disease beginning in adulthood, with fluctuating, fatigable skeletal-muscle weakness caused by impaired postsynaptic neuromuscular transmission. Onset-age groups overlap with antibody-defined and thymic subgroups. Early-onset AChR-positive disease often has thymic follicular hyperplasia, whereas late-onset disease more often accompanies an involuted thymus and striational antibodies. These are population patterns, not obligatory features or established distinct initiating mechanisms. AChR antibodies can activate complement, accelerate receptor loss, or interfere with receptor function; predominantly IgG4 MuSK antibodies impair receptor-clustering signals through a different mechanism. Treatment depends on antibody status, clinical activity, thymic pathology, comorbidity, and age.

📘

Definitions

1
Orphanet disease definition
Orphanet defines adult-onset myasthenia gravis as a rare autoimmune disorder of the neuromuscular junction with fatigable muscle weakness, frequent ocular signs and/or generalized weakness, and occasional thymoma association.
CASE_DEFINITION
Show evidence (1 reference)
ORPHA:391490 SUPPORT DIRECT BACKGROUND Other
"A rare autoimmune disorder of the neuromuscular junction characterized by fatigable muscle weakness with frequent ocular signs and/or generalized muscle weakness, and occasionally associated with thymoma."
Orphanet's definition for the adult-onset subtype anchors the scope of this entry.
◆

Subtypes

3
Early-Onset MG (onset before ~50 years)
Adult-onset disease before the study-specific late-onset boundary, commonly 50 years. Female predominance and follicular thymic hyperplasia are especially associated with AChR-positive early-onset cohorts. Research labeled EOMG may include juvenile cases, so its results are not automatically adult-specific.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:40675735 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"EOMG often presents with distinct immunological and genetic profiles, a predominance of female patients, and a higher incidence of thymic hyperplasia."
Defines the early-onset subgroup by its immunogenetic profile, sex skew, and thymic pathology.
Late-Onset MG (onset at or after ~50 years)
Disease beginning at or beyond the study-specific late-onset boundary, commonly 50 years. AChR-positive disease, male predominance, ocular presentation, and anti-titin antibodies are relatively enriched in several cohorts. An atrophic thymus is common but does not prove immunological inactivity or an exclusively extrathymic origin.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:33458590 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"LOMG with onset ≥ 50 years of age is more common in men and ocular onset is common. Frequency of anti-AChR and anti-titin antibodies are high."
Gives the conventional ≥50-year onset boundary together with the male skew and antibody profile that define the subgroup.
PMID:40675735 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"In contrast, LOMG and VLOMG are characterized by thymic atrophy, differing genetic associations, and milder long-term disease courses with variable treatment responses."
Contrasts late-onset thymic and genetic biology with the early-onset subgroup.
Very Late-Onset MG (onset after ~65 years)
A further age stratum within late-onset disease, often defined as onset at or beyond 65 years. Focal ocular or bulbar presentations can occur; comorbidity, diagnostic alternatives, and treatment tolerability warrant particular attention. The threshold varies between reports.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23893883 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"We define late-onset myasthenia gravis (LOMG) when symptoms appear at ≥65 years of age. There has been a continuous increase in the incidence of LOMG with a clear male predominance."
Documents the ≥65-year boundary used for the very-late-onset tier — and, by differing from the ≥50-year boundary used elsewhere, shows that the cut-point itself is a convention rather than a measured discontinuity.
PMID:23893883 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Commonly, patients present with focal (ocular or bulbar) weakness. A high index of suspicion required to achieve early diagnosis and to improve prognosis."
Supports focal presentation and diagnostic delay as characteristic of the oldest-onset group.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

3
Onset-associated genetic architecture and ascertainment
INTERPRETATION OPEN adult-mg-interpretation-1
Onset-age strata show differing HLA and non-HLA associations, with shared susceptibility and substantial ascertainment limits. Older studies were enriched for European AChR-positive, nonthymomatous disease and early-onset cohorts could include adolescents. The 2024 discovery analysis included 5,708 cases and 432,028 controls and reused prior European cohorts; replication used 3,989 self-reported cases and 226,643 controls. Its early-onset replication used age below 50 at participation, while late-onset replication used all cases because onset age was unavailable. These results do not prove that the age strata are distinct diseases or establish clinical diagnostic use of polygenic scores.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:39537604 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"We defined EOMG cases as those under 50 years old at participation."
The replication proxy differs from measured onset age.
PMID:39537604 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"As no age of onset information was available, we replicated the LOMG discovery GWAS loci using the full 23andMe MG dataset."
The late-onset replication was not restricted to verified late-onset cases.
PMID:34400559 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"MG onset-specific analysis reveals differences in the genetic architecture of early-onset MG (EOMG) versus late-onset MG (LOMG)."
The abstract supports differences, not complete genetic separation.
Striational antibodies and associated muscle or cardiac inflammation
INTERPRETATION OPEN adult-mg-interpretation-2
Myositis and myocarditis can accompany MG, particularly in selected thymoma-associated or late-onset series. A 2019 study analyzed stored sera from 30 patients selected for MG with myositis and/or myocarditis; it was not a population-incidence study. Titin and Kv1.4 antibodies were common in that selected group, while RyR reactivity lacked specificity. These assays do not establish that the antibodies caused muscle or cardiac injury, and myocarditis mortality must not be presented as the mortality of myasthenic crisis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:30918333 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"MG patients with myositis and/or myocarditis as well as late-onset and thymoma-associated MG had anti-titin, anti-ryanodine receptor, and anti-Kv1.4 antibodies."
Primary selected-sample assay findings; no general frequency or pathogenic function is inferred.
Emerging thymic cell-interaction hypotheses
EMERGING HYPOTHESIS OPEN adult-mg-interpretation-3
Single-cell and BCR profiling of untreated early-onset MG thymi identified clonal expansion and inferred cell-state trajectories and MIF–CD74 communication. Spatial proximity and protein staining support the tissue context but do not demonstrate signaling necessity, lineage ancestry, or response to MIF blockade. Serum MIF associations are exploratory and are not a validated diagnostic or disease-activity test.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:40117520 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Clonally expanded sequences were most enriched in the GC and cycling B-cell subsets"
Measured clonal enrichment in the primary dataset; antigen specificity and proposed communication require further validation.
⚙

Pathophysiology

18
Thymic Chemokine Overexpression
CXCL13 and CCL21 overexpression in hyperplastic AChR-positive MG thymus is associated with recruitment of peripheral lymphocytes. This is an early-onset-enriched pathway; its initiating stimulus is unresolved.
thymus UBERON:0002370 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in thymus (UBERON:0002370). UBERON:0002370 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Downstream
Thymic Lymphocyte Recruitment Chemokines favor peripheral lymphocyte recruitment into the inflammatory thymus.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"B cell infiltration and germinal center formation is associated with overexpression of CXCL13, CCL21, and B cell–activating factor (BAFF) in thymic epithelial cells"
Review synthesis of thymic chemokine findings.
Thymic Lymphocyte Recruitment
Peripheral B cells and helper T cells accumulate in the hyperplastic thymus, with vascular changes that support cellular entry.

Downstream
Ectopic Thymic Germinal Center Formation Recruited lymphocytes support ectopic germinal-center organization.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:29125185 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The hyperplastic MG thymus displays all the characteristics of tertiary lymphoid organs (TLOs): neoangiogenic processes with high endothelial venule and lymphatic vessel development, chemokine overexpression favoring peripheral cell recruitment, and ectopic germinal center development."
Thymic cellular recruitment described in a mechanistic review.
Ectopic Thymic Germinal Center Formation
Ectopic thymic follicles provide a niche for B-cell clonal expansion and maturation, particularly in AChR-positive early-onset disease. A 2025 single-cell study analyzed 11 immunotherapy-naive thymi after one of 12 recruited samples failed quality control; inferred trajectories are not lineage tracing.

Downstream
AChR Autoantibody Production The thymic germinal-center environment can sustain AChR-reactive B-lineage cells; circulating antibody-producing cells also maintain disease after thymectomy.
Thymic Follicular Hyperplasia Ectopic follicles contribute to the histological hyperplasia.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:40117520 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Clonally expanded sequences were most enriched in the GC and cycling B-cell subsets"
Primary BCR sequencing supports local clonal expansion, not antigen specificity of every expanded clone.
PMID:38886398 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Compared to other studies, the notably high incidence of thymectomy in MG patients from our database can be attributed to the fact that our cohort was mainly collected from a specialized cardio-thoracic surgical center."
The French histology cohort is surgically selected, so its germinal-center frequencies are not population frequencies.
Thymoma-Associated Loss of Immune Tolerance
In thymoma-associated disease, abnormal T-cell selection and export of autoreactive T cells can support AChR autoimmunity. Tumor histology and immune changes vary; no single molecular lesion is asserted for every thymoma.
thymus UBERON:0002370 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in thymus (UBERON:0002370). UBERON:0002370 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Downstream
AChR Autoantibody Production Autoreactive T-cell help can support antibody-producing B cells outside the tumor, permitting persistent or delayed disease after resection.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Development of MG requires the generation of autoreactive T cells within the tumor and subsequent exit of these T cells to the periphery and is further associated with a reduction of Tregs"
Review synthesis of the thymoma-associated mechanism.
AChR Autoantibody Production
Autoreactive B-lineage cells produce AChR-directed antibodies in the thymus and peripheral compartments. Antibody titer alone does not determine pathogenic activity or clinical severity.

Downstream
AChR Autoantibody Binding Circulating antibodies with accessible extracellular epitopes bind the muscle receptor; not every detected antibody is pathogenic.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Thymocytes produce AChR antibodies, as do circulating plasma cells"
Review identifies intrathymic and circulating sources.
AChR Autoantibody Binding
AChR-reactive antibodies bind the postsynaptic receptor. Isotype, epitope, combinations of antibodies, and tissue access affect their ability to cause injury.

Downstream
Complement Activation at the Endplate Complement-fixing antibody combinations recruit the complement cascade.
AChR Crosslinking Bivalent antibodies and complementary epitope specificities can crosslink receptors.
AChR Functional Blockade Some antibodies interfere with receptor activation independently of complement-mediated injury.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36074148 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"All AChR-specific antibodies were hypermutated, including isotypes IgG1, IgG3, and IgG4, and recognized different subunits of the AChR."
Patient-derived monoclonal antibodies characterize binding specificity in experimental assays.
Complement Activation at the Endplate
Complement-fixing AChR antibodies promote terminal complement activation and membrane attack complex formation at the postsynaptic membrane. This is distinct from the predominant IgG4 MuSK mechanism.

Animal models

Downstream
Postsynaptic Membrane Injury Membrane attack complex formation injures receptor-rich membrane and synaptic folds.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Upon binding of AChR antibody, the membrane attack complex is formed on the postsynaptic surface and leads to the shedding of AChR-rich membrane resulting in the loss of synaptic folding as well as Na+ channels"
Established complement mechanism, with variable contribution between patients.
Postsynaptic Membrane Injury
Complement-associated injury removes receptor-rich postsynaptic membrane and simplifies junctional architecture. Loss of sodium channels also reduces the transmission safety factor.

Reduced Endplate Potential Safety Margin Postsynaptic structural and sodium-channel loss make generation of a muscle action potential less reliable.

Review describes receptor-rich membrane shedding and channel loss.
AChR Crosslinking
Antibodies can crosslink AChR molecules into abnormal surface assemblies, distinct from physiological receptor clustering. Patient-derived antibody combinations showed greater clustering than individual antibodies in transfected cells.

Accelerated AChR Internalization
Antigenic modulation accelerates endocytosis and degradation of antibody-crosslinked receptors.

AChR Functional Blockade
Some AChR antibodies inhibit receptor activation without requiring complement. Patient sera inhibited neurotransmission in an iPSC-derived system after heat inactivation, but the six cloned antibodies in that study did not reproduce blockade, either individually or in tested combinations.

MuSK Autoantibody Binding
Predominantly IgG4 MuSK antibodies bind extracellular MuSK epitopes. This serologic branch is not confined to one adult age group and does not require complement activation as its principal mechanism.

Impaired LRP4-MuSK Interaction Patient IgG4 antibodies block association between LRP4 and MuSK.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24297891 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT In Vitro
"Here we show that pathogenic IgG4 antibodies to MuSK bind to a structural epitope in the first Ig-like domain of MuSK, prevent binding between MuSK and Lrp4, and inhibit Agrin-stimulated MuSK phosphorylation."
Purified human patient antibodies were tested with recombinant proteins and cultured cells.
Impaired LRP4-MuSK Interaction
MuSK IgG4 binding impedes the agrin-dependent interaction between LRP4 and MuSK. The 2013 experiments did not show a direct effect on MuSK homodimerization or surface abundance in the engineered fibroblast context.

Reduced Agrin-Stimulated MuSK Phosphorylation
MuSK-directed IgG4 reduces agrin-stimulated MuSK phosphorylation in cultured myotubes, impairing the signaling that organizes the postsynaptic receptor apparatus.

Impaired Postsynaptic AChR Clustering
MuSK-pathway disruption reduces proper accumulation of AChRs at the postsynaptic muscle membrane. This differs from antibody-induced crosslinking of receptors in AChR-positive disease.

"MuSK antibodies interfere with clustering of AChR on the postsynaptic muscle surface directly opposed to the nerve terminal."
Review-supported MuSK pathway effect.
Reduced Endplate Potential Safety Margin
The margin between endplate depolarization and the threshold for a muscle action potential is reduced. Repetitive activation can then make transmission fail in individual fibers.

Impaired Neuromuscular Transmission During repetitive activity, endplate depolarization can fall below the action-potential threshold.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Neuromuscular transmission failure occurs owing to a reduction in the number or activity of AChR molecules at the NMJ, leading to a decrease in the end-plate potential (EPP)."
Review connects reduced receptor number/activity to endplate potential.
Impaired Neuromuscular Transmission
Motor-nerve activation increasingly fails to elicit muscle-fiber action potentials and contraction, producing fatigable weakness in variably affected skeletal-muscle groups.
neuromuscular synaptic transmission GO:0007274 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves neuromuscular synaptic transmission (GO:0007274). GO:0007274 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
neuromuscular junction GO:0031594 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cellular component This pathophysiological event involves neuromuscular junction (GO:0031594). GO:0031594 is a cellular component from the Gene Ontology.
skeletal muscle tissue UBERON:0001134 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in skeletal muscle tissue (UBERON:0001134). UBERON:0001134 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Animal models
Patient-Derived AChR Antibody Passive Transfer in Lewis Rats Rattus norvegicus partially recapitulates The combination caused a weakness phenotype, providing an indirect functional readout of junction injury.

"Neuromuscular fatigue, characterized by a progressive loss of force generation, occurs as increasing numbers of muscle fibers become incapable of contracting."
Shared downstream physiology of antibody-mediated MG.

Medical Actions

26
Pyridostigmine
Category: Therapeutic Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: Pyridostigmine CHEBI:8665 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses Pyridostigmine (CHEBI:8665). CHEBI:8665 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Acetylcholinesterase inhibition increases acetylcholine availability for symptomatic improvement without suppressing autoantibody production. MuSK-positive patients may respond poorly or tolerate it poorly; treatment is individualized.
Mechanism Target:
MODULATES Impaired Neuromuscular Transmission — Prolongs acetylcholine availability and improves the transmission safety margin.
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK559331/ SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Acetylcholinesterase inhibitors increases the level of ACh at the NMJ by preventing its enzymatic degradation."
Direct pharmacological rationale.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"For symptomatic therapy of MG, acetylcholinesterase inhibitors (AChE-I), primarily pyridostigmine, should be used."
Symptomatic recommendation.
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Patients with MuSK-Ab-positive MG may respond poorly to AChE-I and more often tolerate it poorly."
Important serotype-specific limitation.
Corticosteroids
Category: Therapeutic Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: prednisone CHEBI:8382 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses prednisone (CHEBI:8382). CHEBI:8382 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Glucocorticoids are basic immunotherapy for inadequately controlled ocular or generalized MG. Use the lowest effective exposure with monitoring for infection, metabolic, bone, and other toxicity; early transient worsening and comorbidity influence initiation and bridging care.
Mechanism Target:
MODULATES AChR Autoantibody Production — Broad immune suppression modifies disease activity; the clinical response does not identify a single cellular mediator.

Supports disease-modifying immunotherapy.

Guideline frames benefit against treatment burden.
Azathioprine
Category: Therapeutic Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: azathioprine CHEBI:2948 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses azathioprine (CHEBI:2948). CHEBI:2948 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
A steroid-sparing immunosuppressant with delayed onset over months. Clinical and laboratory monitoring is necessary. Favorable outcomes in a late-onset observational cohort support applicability to older-onset patients but do not isolate a randomized combination effect.
Mechanism Target:
MODULATES AChR Autoantibody Production — Immunosuppression reduces disease activity through multiple immune processes.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"GKS as monotherapy or in combination with AZA are first-choice agents for immunotherapy."
Disease-modifying use; no claim of selective plasma-cell removal.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Due to the slow onset of action, therapeutic success with monotherapy is not to be expected before several months have elapsed."
Delayed azathioprine response in the relevant guideline section.
PMID:31811563 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Use of prednisone + azathioprine had significantly positive effect on outcome."
Association in an observational late-onset cohort.

Thymectomy for Nonthymomatous AChR-Positive Generalized MG
Category: Therapeutic Action: ThymectomyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Thymectomy (NCIT:C29894). NCIT:C29894 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C29894
Platform: Surgery
MGTX randomized 126 adults aged 18–65 with AChR-positive generalized nonthymomatous MG of less than five years duration to extended transsternal thymectomy plus prednisone or prednisone alone. Surgery improved the three-year time-weighted QMG score and reduced steroid requirements. Older adults were included; age-subgroup analyses do not establish benefit exclusively in early-onset disease. Evidence for very old patients, long-standing disease, pure ocular disease, and other surgical approaches is less direct. MuSK-positive MG without a tumor is not an immunological indication.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Ectopic Thymic Germinal Center Formation — Removes the thymic tissue harboring ectopic follicles; persistent peripheral autoreactive cells can sustain disease.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The precise mechanism by which thymus removal imparts clinical benefit is not fully understood."
Clinical benefit does not prove a single mediator.
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT INDIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"One likely hypothesis is that thymectomy eliminates a considerable source of antigenic stimulation, ultimately reducing the production of AChR antibodies, but autoreactive cells have exited the thymus remain and drive production of pathogenic antibodies"
Supports removal of a thymic antigenic source while qualifying the mediator and persistent peripheral activity.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:27509100 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Patients 18 to 65 years of age who had generalized nonthymomatous myasthenia gravis with a disease duration of less than 5 years were included"
Enrollment age is not a proof of exclusively early-onset disease.
PMID:27509100 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Patients who underwent thymectomy had a lower time-weighted average Quantitative Myasthenia Gravis score over a 3-year period than those who received prednisone alone (6.15 vs. 8.99, P<0.001)"
Randomized clinical outcome.
PMID:40022458 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Thymectomy may be a potentially effective treatment for LOMG, particularly in patients who undergo the procedure soon after diagnosis. A randomized controlled study for LOMG patients is needed."
Mostly observational meta-analysis supports continued uncertainty for a dedicated late-onset treatment effect.
Thymoma-Directed Treatment
Category: Therapeutic Action: ThymectomyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Thymectomy (NCIT:C29894). NCIT:C29894 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C29894
Platform: Surgery
Assess and manage thymoma according to tumor stage and operability, generally with surgical removal when feasible. This oncological indication applies independently of MG severity or onset-age category; removal does not guarantee resolution of MG.

Rituximab
Category: Therapeutic Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: Rituximab NCIT:C1702 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses Rituximab (NCIT:C1702). NCIT:C1702 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Monoclonal antibody
CD20-directed B-cell depletion is particularly supported for MuSK-positive MG. AChR-positive trial results depend on population and endpoint: BeatMG missed its primary steroid-sparing endpoint, whereas RINOMAX supported benefit in new-onset disease. Infusion, infection, and immunoglobulin risks require specialist monitoring; no efficacy ranking against other targeted drugs is inferred.
Mechanism Target:
MODULATES AChR Autoantibody Production — B-cell depletion can reduce replenishment of antibody-producing cells; long-lived plasma cells are not uniformly depleted.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Rituximab is a monoclonal chimeric Ab that specifically binds to the marker CD20 on the membrane of pre-B and mature B lymphocytes and causes transient B-cell depletion"
The direct target is the B-cell compartment, with downstream antibody effects.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Rituximab is a monoclonal chimeric Ab that specifically binds to the marker CD20 on the membrane of pre-B and mature B lymphocytes and causes transient B-cell depletion"
Mechanism summarized in the guideline.

"The recently completed randomized-controlled phase III RINOMAX trial demonstrated that patients with new-onset AChR-Ab-positive gMG on a low-dose rituximab regimen (one infusion of 500 mg) were significantly more likely to meet the primary end point of minimal disease manifestation at 16 weeks..."
New-onset trial context; not all refractory MG populations.

Vaccination and Infection-Risk Management
Category: Therapeutic
Review vaccination and infection risks before immunotherapy. Complement inhibition requires meningococcal precautions and, where urgent initiation precludes adequate vaccine protection, antibiotic prophylaxis according to the applicable regimen. Live vaccines are generally avoided during immunotherapy. Protection is not complete after vaccination.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"If the effectiveness of the vaccination cannot be waited for or if a vaccination success cannot be assumed due to the immunotherapy, prophylactic antibiotic therapy should be started in addition to the vaccination."
Guideline complement-inhibitor precaution.
Clinical and Treatment-Toxicity Monitoring
Category: Monitoring
Assess clinical strength, function, MG-ADL or related scales, quality of life, and treatment toxicity over time. Routine antibody titers should not substitute for clinical disease-activity assessment.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Therefore, routine clinical use of autoantibody testing to determine disease activity cannot be recommended."
Correlation is too variable for routine activity monitoring.
Fatigue Management and Rehabilitation
Category: Therapeutic
Assess persistent fatigue separately from muscle weakness, optimize disease control and sleep, and support appropriately paced exercise, nutrition, and psychological care. Fatigue-drug evidence is limited.
Mechanism Target:
MODULATES Fatigue — Addresses contributors to perceived fatigue and function.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Patients should be encouraged to not overprotect themselves or exert themselves beyond the limits set by MG."
Individual pacing rather than unrestricted exercise.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Patients’ self-efficacy should be enhanced via modification of lifestyle factors, such as good sleep hygiene, balanced diet, avoidance of excessive alcohol consumption, and performance of aerobic exercise. If necessary, additional psychological support may be helpful."
Guideline supportive care.
Pregnancy and Reproductive Counseling
Category: Counseling / Informational
Arrange specialist counseling before or during pregnancy, review drug-specific fetal risks and treatment alternatives, and coordinate maternal and neonatal care. Transient neonatal disease from maternal antibody transfer is distinct from inherited congenital myasthenic syndromes.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"In case of pregnancy and a desire to have children, a specific consultation should be made in a center specialized in myasthenia."
Specialist counseling recommendation.
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Because of the risk of neonatal MG and peripartum deterioration of maternal MG, child delivery should be planned in a maximum care hospital, including those with neonatology and neuropediatric expertise and intensive care monitoring capabilities."
Supports coordinated delivery and neonatal planning.
Medication and Exacerbation Education
Category: Counseling / Informational
Review new medications for possible worsening, discuss infection and intercurrent-illness plans, and teach recognition of bulbar or respiratory deterioration. Medication risk is contextual and should not be replaced by a blanket prohibition of every drug reported to worsen MG.
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK559331/ SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"take their medications as directed and to avoid taking new medicines without checking with the medical provider."
Medication review and adherence advice.
🔬

Biochemical Markers

4
Anti-Acetylcholine Receptor Antibody
Context: AChR antibodies are diagnostically useful in a compatible clinical presentation. Detection rates vary with clinical distribution, assay, and cohort; antibody concentration does not reliably quantify current disease activity.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:22476514 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Anti-AChR antibody titers were elevated in 78 % of patients (65 % with EOMG vs. 85 % with LOMG; p = 0.003)"
Gives the onset-stratified seropositivity rates in a single cohort tested identically.
Anti-Titin Antibody
Context: Titin antibodies are associated with older onset and thymoma in selected cohorts, but are not established causes of neuromuscular transmission failure. A retrospective series found an association with generalized disease within its late-onset subgroup; this is not a validated prediction of progression.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:31811563 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In 95 patients with generalized non-thymomatous LOMG, 60 (63%) were men, 45 (47%) had mild disease, 80 (84%) were anti-AChR, and 56 (61%) were anti-titin positive."
Quantifies anti-titin seropositivity at 61% in generalized non-thymomatous late-onset disease.
PMID:38505076 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Anti-titin positivity was significantly associated with generalized MG in the late-onset group (P=0.005)."
Establishes the late-onset-specific clinical correlate of anti-titin positivity.
Anti-Ryanodine Receptor Antibody
Context: RyR reactivity occurs in MG with striational autoimmunity, but the 2019 cell-based assay also detected substantial reactivity in disease controls and healthy controls. It is neither diagnostic of late-onset MG nor proof of pathogenicity.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:30918333 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"MG patients with myositis and/or myocarditis as well as late-onset and thymoma-associated MG had anti-titin, anti-ryanodine receptor, and anti-Kv1.4 antibodies."
Places anti-ryanodine-receptor antibody within the striational panel of late-onset and thymoma-associated disease.
PMID:30918333 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"In contrast, patients with early-onset MG, those with other myopathies and healthy controls did not have anti-titin or anti-Kv1.4 antibodies with some exceptions, but they possessed anti-ryanodine receptor antibodies."
The control reactivity supports the stated specificity limitation.
Anti-MuSK Antibody
Context: MuSK antibodies identify an antibody-mediated subgroup with different effector mechanisms and treatment responses. Negative AChR serology alone does not establish MuSK disease.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:33458590 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Anti-MuSK antibodies are extremely rare in MG with prolonged pure ocular symptoms."
Subtype and clinical-distribution context, not a universal frequency.
🔬

Diagnosis

5
Clinical assessment with confirmatory testing
Identify fatigable, fluctuating weakness and its ocular, bulbar, respiratory, axial, or limb distribution. Confirm a compatible syndrome with antibody testing and/or neuromuscular electrophysiology; exclude mimics when the pattern or treatment response is atypical.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The diagnosis of MG is based on the history and physical findings of fatigable and fluctuating muscle weakness. The diagnosis is confirmed by positive findings in auto-Ab diagnostics and/or electrophysiology and/or pharmacological testing."
Guideline diagnostic framework.
Serological subtyping
Test AChR and, where appropriate, MuSK and other antibodies using validated assays. Cell-based AChR testing can identify some conventionally seronegative patients. LRP4 and striational antibody findings require clinical context; they do not independently establish MG.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Recently, cell-based assays have been developed with plasma membrane expression of the AChR, allowing the antigen to resemble the native receptor more closely"
Describes improved antigen presentation.
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"although they are also present in patients with motor neuron disease and patients without evidence of diseases"
Limits LRP4 specificity.
Repetitive nerve stimulation and single-fiber electromyography
Electrophysiology supports impaired neuromuscular transmission, especially when serology is negative. Sensitivity depends on muscles tested and phenotype. SFEMG is sensitive but nonspecific; abnormal limb jitter in ocular MG does not by itself prove clinical generalization.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:33458590 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Repetitive nerve stimulation is not very useful with purely ocular symptoms. Single fiber EMG is very sensitive, but one has to be careful remembering that it is not specific to MG"
Test-specific limitations.
PMID:33458590 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Presence of abnormalities in limb muscles of a patient with OMG does not indicate generalization"
Clinical distribution cannot be inferred solely from abnormal limb jitter.
Thymoma assessment
Evaluate the mediastinum with chest imaging for thymoma. A negative striational-antibody test does not replace imaging.
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK559331/ SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Chest computed tomography (CT) or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) should be performed in patients diagnosed with MG to assess for thymoma."
Explicit imaging recommendation.
Review of seronegative and atypical presentations
Consider congenital myasthenic syndromes, other neuromuscular disorders, and structural ocular or brainstem causes when appropriate. Adult presentation does not exclude a congenital myasthenic syndrome; genetic testing addresses that differential, not routine confirmation of autoimmune MG.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"An important, although rare, differential diagnosis of autoimmune myasthenic syndromes – not only in children but also in adults – is the congenital myasthenic syndromes"
Adult differential diagnosis.
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"If CMS is suspected, molecular genetic diagnostics must be performed to confirm the diagnosis and plan therapy."
Genetic testing is conditional on a suspected inherited syndrome.
📈

Progression

3
Adult onset and fluctuating course
Age: Adulthood
Symptoms fluctuate with activity and over time. Early-, late-, and very-late-onset cutoffs vary across studies and do not define obligatory thymic pathology.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39105625 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The hallmark of MG is muscle fatigue with a degree of weakness that can fluctuate over minutes and vary in severity over weeks to months."
Describes the variable course.
Ocular disease and possible generalization
Ocular disease may remain restricted or progress to nonocular weakness. Generalization is most often observed within the first two years but can occur later; electrophysiological abnormalities alone do not define clinical generalization.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:33458590 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Generalization usually occurs within one year, mostly within 2 years and it is uncommon after 2 years"
Timing is a common pattern, not an absolute two-year cutoff.
PMID:33458590 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Myasthenia generalized after 2 years in another small group of patients."
Primary cohort observations in the review document later progression.
Exacerbation and crisis
Respiratory or severe bulbar weakness can deteriorate rapidly and require monitored or intensive care. Infection, intercurrent illness, medication effects, and withdrawal of effective treatment can precipitate worsening.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"In the event of an impending and manifest myasthenic crisis, rapid admission and treatment must be provided in a monitoring or intensive care unit with experience in neuromuscular disease."
Guideline defines the urgent care setting.
PMID:38152089 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Abrupt discontinuation of immunotherapy in an insufficiently stabilized state is risky and should be avoided as it may lead to the recurrence of myasthenic symptoms and can escalate to a myasthenic crisis."
Treatment withdrawal is a potentially preventable precipitant.
🌍

Epidemiology

2
Increasing late-onset incidence in a Danish population
A population study of nonthymomatous AChR-seropositive MG in Denmark during 1970–1999 found stable early-onset incidence and rising late-onset incidence. These historical, serotype-specific rates are not a contemporary global incidence estimate for all adult-onset MG.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:16186537 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"The mean annual incidence rate of early-onset MG was constant at 3.5 x 10(-6). In late-onset MG, the rate increased from 4.7 to 20.8 x 10(-6)."
Historical population observation; not proof of a particular environmental cause.
Contemporary MG prevalence context
A 2025 epidemiology review summarizes approximately 20 incident cases per million person-years and 25 prevalent cases per 100,000 for MG overall. These are not measured adult-onset-only estimates. Population aging, improved ascertainment, and survival contribute to increasing prevalence.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:40675734 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Recent high-quality studies have found an annual MG incidence of approximately 20 per million person-years, and a prevalence of approximately 25 per 100,000 population."
All-MG context, deliberately not encoded as a prevalence band for this subtype.
PMID:40675734 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"MG prevalence has increased in recent years because of more elderly people in the general population, improved case finding, and improved MG treatment with reduced mortality."
The review identifies multiple contributors rather than a single biological cause.

Related Datasets

1
Thymic B lineage cell landscape in Myasthenia gravis geo:GSE233180
Single-cell transcriptome and BCR data from immunotherapy-naive AChR-positive early-onset MG thymi. Twelve patients were recruited; one sample failed quality control, and the final reported atlas comprised 29,688 cells from 11 patients. Repository sample records and recruited patients are not interchangeable denominators.

Accession verified with just verify-datasets; the per-record GEO cache is the verification artifact. Cell trajectories and MIF–CD74 communication are computational inferences, not lineage-tracing or signaling-intervention experiments.

Clinical Trials

7
NCT00294658 PHASE_III COMPLETED
MGTX compared extended transsternal thymectomy plus standardized prednisone with prednisone alone in 126 adults with generalized nonthymomatous AChR-positive MG of less than five years duration. The three-year randomized results support clinical benefit and steroid sparing. Enrollment age 18–65 included older adults; this is not a dedicated, adequately powered very-late-onset trial.
Target Phenotypes: Generalized Muscle Weakness HP:0003324 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical trial targets this phenotype This clinical trial targets Generalized Muscle Weakness (HP:0003324). HP:0003324 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (2 references)
clinicaltrials:NCT00294658 SUPPORT DIRECT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"The purpose of this trial is to determine if thymectomy combined with prednisone therapy is more beneficial in treating non-thymomatous myasthenia gravis than prednisone therapy alone."
Registry design statement; not clinical outcome evidence.
PMID:27509100 SUPPORT DIRECT PRIMARY RESULT Human Clinical
"Patients who underwent thymectomy had a lower time-weighted average Quantitative Myasthenia Gravis score over a 3-year period than those who received prednisone alone (6.15 vs. 8.99, P<0.001)"
Primary randomized clinical outcome.
NCT04524273 PHASE_III ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING
MINT tested inebilizumab against placebo with background therapy in 238 adults, including 190 AChR-positive and 48 MuSK-positive participants. The primary pooled week-26 MG-ADL endpoint favored treatment by 1.9 points. The randomized periods differed by serotype, with an extension; an active registry status does not mean the published primary outcome is pending.

Experimental Models

4
AChR-Transfected HEK Antibody-Combination Assays CELL_LINE
Complement deposition and live-cell receptor clustering were measured after exposure to individual antibodies or equal-total-concentration combinations. Epitope combinations showed synergy in the tested panel. The proposed two-dimensional receptor-antibody lattice is a hypothesis; imaging of surface clustering does not itself quantify internalization or prove a universal requirement for multiple epitopes in patients.

Human iPSC-Derived Neuromuscular Coculture with MG Sera IPSC_DERIVED_MODEL
Neuronal AMPA stimulation and myotube calcium imaging assessed functional transmission after serum exposure. MG sera inhibited the response after heat inactivation, supporting a complement-independent component. The six cloned antibodies and their tested combinations did not reproduce direct blockade. The assay tests serum effects in an engineered junction, not spontaneous adult-onset autoimmunity or the precise blocking epitope.

Patient MuSK IgG4 in Recombinant Binding and Myotube Assays OTHER
IgG4 fractions from seven patients blocked recombinant LRP4–MuSK binding in a dose-dependent manner, whereas matched IgG1–3 and control antibodies had smaller effects. Patient IgG4 also inhibited agrin-stimulated MuSK phosphorylation in C2 myotubes. The broader cohort included 25 Dutch patients; this does not establish identical epitope or isotype behavior in every population.

Animal Models

1
Patient-Derived AChR Antibody Passive Transfer in Lewis Rats Passive antibody transfer
A single injection of aG101 plus bG402-G1 induced weakness, complement deposition, receptor loss, and junction fragmentation. Total antibody dose was matched to individual-antibody arms; behavior was assessed in 6–7 animals per group pooled from two experiments. A separate early histology cohort assessed complement and morphology. Single antibodies did not produce the overt behavioral phenotype, although aG101 alone increased C3 staining.
Species
Rattus norvegicus
Background
Four-week-old female Lewis rats
Publication
PMID:36074148
Pathograph links
Complement Activation at the Endplate partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate The antibody combination increased junctional C3 staining beyond either control or aG101 alone.
Limitations: Acute passive transfer into four-week-old female Lewis rats bypasses human loss of tolerance and antibody production. bG402 was artificially switched from IgG4 to IgG1. The model does not test aging, late-onset initiation, chronic disease, or MuSK autoimmunity.

Source YAML

click to show 
name: Adult-Onset Myasthenia Gravis
creation_date: '2026-08-19T09:00:00Z'
description: Adult-onset myasthenia gravis is acquired autoimmune disease beginning in adulthood, with fluctuating, fatigable skeletal-muscle weakness caused by impaired postsynaptic neuromuscular transmission. Onset-age groups overlap with antibody-defined and thymic subgroups. Early-onset AChR-positive disease often has thymic follicular hyperplasia, whereas late-onset disease more often accompanies an involuted thymus and striational antibodies. These are population patterns, not obligatory features or established distinct initiating mechanisms. AChR antibodies can activate complement, accelerate receptor loss, or interfere with receptor function; predominantly IgG4 MuSK antibodies impair receptor-clustering signals through a different mechanism. Treatment depends on antibody status, clinical activity, thymic pathology, comorbidity, and age.
category: Autoimmune
synonyms:
- adult-onset acquired myasthenia
- adult-onset autoimmune myasthenia gravis
parents:
- Myasthenia Gravis
- Neuromuscular Disease
- Autoimmune Disease
disease_term:
  preferred_term: adult-onset myasthenia gravis
  term:
    id: MONDO:0018324
    label: adult-onset myasthenia gravis
notes: 'This entry covers adult-onset autoimmune MG, complementing the broader Myasthenia Gravis entry. Adult onset is not synonymous with onset after puberty: juvenile cohorts include postpubertal patients below 18 years. Studies use different early/late boundaries, commonly 40–50 years; very-late-onset disease is a subset of late-onset disease, often defined from 65 years. Some cited early-onset cohorts include adolescents, and trials enrolling adults do not necessarily establish adult age at disease onset. Age categories are clinical strata, not causal exposures. Shared antibody mechanisms and care are included where needed to explain the adult presentation and treatment choices.'

    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: EOMG often presents with distinct immunological and genetic profiles, a predominance of female patients, and a higher incidence of thymic hyperplasia.
    explanation: Defines the early-onset subgroup by its immunogenetic profile, sex skew, and thymic pathology.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
- name: LOMG
  display_name: Late-Onset MG (onset at or after ~50 years)
  description: Disease beginning at or beyond the study-specific late-onset boundary, commonly 50 years. AChR-positive disease, male predominance, ocular presentation, and anti-titin antibodies are relatively enriched in several cohorts. An atrophic thymus is common but does not prove immunological inactivity or an exclusively extrathymic origin.
  subtype_term:
    preferred_term: late-onset myasthenia gravis
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:33458590
    reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: MuSK MG, late-onset MG and ocular MG.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: LOMG with onset ≥ 50 years of age is more common in men and ocular onset is common. Frequency of anti-AChR and anti-titin antibodies are high.
    explanation: Gives the conventional ≥50-year onset boundary together with the male skew and antibody profile that define the subgroup.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
  - reference: PMID:40675735
    reference_title: 'Special populations in myasthenia gravis: Early, late, and very late-onset MG.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: In contrast, LOMG and VLOMG are characterized by thymic atrophy, differing genetic associations, and milder long-term disease courses with variable treatment responses.
    explanation: Contrasts late-onset thymic and genetic biology with the early-onset subgroup.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
- name: VLOMG
  display_name: Very Late-Onset MG (onset after ~65 years)
  description: A further age stratum within late-onset disease, often defined as onset at or beyond 65 years. Focal ocular or bulbar presentations can occur; comorbidity, diagnostic alternatives, and treatment tolerability warrant particular attention. The threshold varies between reports.
  subtype_term:
    preferred_term: very late-onset myasthenia gravis
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:23893883
    reference_title: 'Late-onset myasthenia gravis: a review when incidence in older adults keeps increasing.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: We define late-onset myasthenia gravis (LOMG) when symptoms appear at ≥65 years of age. There has been a continuous increase in the incidence of LOMG with a clear male predominance.
    explanation: Documents the ≥65-year boundary used for the very-late-onset tier — and, by differing from the ≥50-year boundary used elsewhere, shows that the cut-point itself is a convention rather than a measured discontinuity.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
  - reference: PMID:23893883
    reference_title: 'Late-onset myasthenia gravis: a review when incidence in older adults keeps increasing.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: Commonly, patients present with focal (ocular or bulbar) weakness. A high index of suspicion required to achieve early diagnosis and to improve prognosis.
    explanation: Supports focal presentation and diagnostic delay as characteristic of the oldest-onset group.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
progression:
- phase: Adult onset and fluctuating course
  age_range: Adulthood
  notes: Symptoms fluctuate with activity and over time. Early-, late-, and very-late-onset cutoffs vary across studies and do not define obligatory thymic pathology.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39105625
    reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: the future is here.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: The hallmark of MG is muscle fatigue with a degree of weakness that can fluctuate over minutes and vary in severity over weeks to months.
    explanation: Describes the variable course.
- phase: Ocular disease and possible generalization
  notes: Ocular disease may remain restricted or progress to nonocular weakness. Generalization is most often observed within the first two years but can occur later; electrophysiological abnormalities alone do not define clinical generalization.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:33458590
    reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: MuSK MG, late-onset MG and ocular MG.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Generalization usually occurs within one year, mostly within 2 years and it is uncommon after 2 years
    explanation: Timing is a common pattern, not an absolute two-year cutoff.
  - reference: PMID:33458590
    reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: MuSK MG, late-onset MG and ocular MG.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Myasthenia generalized after 2 years in another small group of patients.
    explanation: Primary cohort observations in the review document later progression.
- phase: Exacerbation and crisis
  notes: Respiratory or severe bulbar weakness can deteriorate rapidly and require monitored or intensive care. Infection, intercurrent illness, medication effects, and withdrawal of effective treatment can precipitate worsening.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38152089
    reference_title: Guideline for the management of myasthenic syndromes.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: In the event of an impending and manifest myasthenic crisis, rapid admission and treatment must be provided in a monitoring or intensive care unit with experience in neuromuscular disease.
    explanation: Guideline defines the urgent care setting.
  - reference: PMID:38152089
    reference_title: Guideline for the management of myasthenic syndromes.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Abrupt discontinuation of immunotherapy in an insufficiently stabilized state is risky and should be avoided as it may lead to the recurrence of myasthenic symptoms and can escalate to a myasthenic crisis.
    explanation: Treatment withdrawal is a potentially preventable precipitant.
pathophysiology:
- name: Thymic Chemokine Overexpression
  description: CXCL13 and CCL21 overexpression in hyperplastic AChR-positive MG thymus is associated with recruitment of peripheral lymphocytes. This is an early-onset-enriched pathway; its initiating stimulus is unresolved.

    explanation: Review synthesis of thymic chemokine findings.

    description: Chemokines favor peripheral lymphocyte recruitment into the inflammatory thymus.

      explanation: Review-supported recruitment mechanism; complete initiating sequence remains unresolved.

- name: Reduced Endplate Potential Safety Margin
  description: The margin between endplate depolarization and the threshold for a muscle action potential is reduced. Repetitive activation can then make transmission fail in individual fibers.
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39105625
    reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: the future is here.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Neuromuscular transmission failure occurs owing to a reduction in the number or activity of AChR molecules at the NMJ, leading to a decrease in the end-plate potential (EPP).
    explanation: Review connects reduced receptor number/activity to endplate potential.
  downstream:
  - target: Impaired Neuromuscular Transmission
    description: During repetitive activity, endplate depolarization can fall below the action-potential threshold.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:39105625
      reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: the future is here.'
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: OTHER
      quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
      directness: DIRECT
      snippet: At rest, this EPP reduction may still adequately support neuromuscular transmission; however, during repetitive activity, when the quantal release of acetylcholine (ACh) is reduced, the EPP may fall below the threshold required to trigger an action potential.
      explanation: Direct physiological explanation of use-dependent transmission failure.

- name: Anti-Titin Antibody
  subtype: LOMG
  context: Titin antibodies are associated with older onset and thymoma in selected cohorts, but are not established causes of neuromuscular transmission failure. A retrospective series found an association with generalized disease within its late-onset subgroup; this is not a validated prediction of progression.
  biomarker_term:

    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: Anti-titin positivity was significantly associated with generalized MG in the late-onset group (P=0.005).
    explanation: Establishes the late-onset-specific clinical correlate of anti-titin positivity.
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    directness: DIRECT
  notes: The 2019 cohort abstract reports 56 positive and 61% within 95 participants without specifying the tested denominator in the excerpt. No pooled frequency is inferred. In the 2024 cohort, 130 of 230 clinic patients were tested; 32 were positive, and testing often occurred long after thymectomy.
- name: Anti-Ryanodine Receptor Antibody
  subtype: LOMG
  context: RyR reactivity occurs in MG with striational autoimmunity, but the 2019 cell-based assay also detected substantial reactivity in disease controls and healthy controls. It is neither diagnostic of late-onset MG nor proof of pathogenicity.
  biomarker_term:
    preferred_term: Anti-ryanodine receptor antibody
    term:
      id: HP:5000047
      label: Anti-ryanodine receptor antibody
  notes: 'No onset-stratified frequency is asserted here: the late-onset cohorts cited in this entry quantified anti-titin but not anti-ryanodine-receptor seropositivity, so a frequency would be unsupported.'
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:30918333
    reference_title: Cytometric cell-based assays for anti-striational antibodies in myasthenia gravis with myositis and/or myocarditis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: MG patients with myositis and/or myocarditis as well as late-onset and thymoma-associated MG had anti-titin, anti-ryanodine receptor, and anti-Kv1.4 antibodies.
    explanation: Places anti-ryanodine-receptor antibody within the striational panel of late-onset and thymoma-associated disease.
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    directness: DIRECT
  - reference: PMID:30918333
    reference_title: Cytometric cell-based assays for anti-striational antibodies in myasthenia gravis with myositis and/or myocarditis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: In contrast, patients with early-onset MG, those with other myopathies and healthy controls did not have anti-titin or anti-Kv1.4 antibodies with some exceptions, but they possessed anti-ryanodine receptor antibodies.
    explanation: The control reactivity supports the stated specificity limitation.
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    directness: DIRECT
- name: Anti-MuSK Antibody
  context: MuSK antibodies identify an antibody-mediated subgroup with different effector mechanisms and treatment responses. Negative AChR serology alone does not establish MuSK disease.

- name: HLA-B*08
  subtype: EOMG
  relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: HLA-B
    term:
      id: hgnc:4932
      label: HLA-B
  features: A major association in North European AChR-positive, nonthymomatous early-onset MG. The 2012 study analyzed 649 cases and 2,596 controls; eligibility allowed onset above 10 years, so it was not exclusively adult-onset. Conditioning on HLA-B*08 removed the marginal DRB1*03:01 signal, but linkage disequilibrium prevents a simple inference of a unique causal allele or demonstrated CD8-cell mechanism.

  features: Onset-associated HLA class II signals differ between cohorts. The 2017 study reports enrichment of DRB1*03 in early-onset and DRB1*01 in late-onset cases. - name: HLA-DQA1
  relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY
  gene_term:

      label: HLA-DQA1
  features: In a European AChR-positive, nonthymomatous case-control study, DQA1*05:01 was associated with lower odds of late-onset disease but higher odds in the previously studied early-onset cohort. Conditional analyses favored DQA1 among class II candidates, without proving that it is the unique causal gene. The 532 late-onset cases and their 449-person onset-at-least-60 subset are one cohort, not independent replication.
  evidence:

- name: TNIP1
  subtype: EOMG
  relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: TNIP1
    term:
      id: hgnc:16903
      label: TNIP1
  features: The p.Pro151Ala variant was associated with early-onset MG in 2012, but its functional effect was not measured. Ubiquitin/NF-kB signaling was a proposed interpretation rather than an established variant mechanism. Failure to detect a signal in the modest 2015 late-onset cohort does not establish universal early-onset specificity; the 2024 meta-analysis also implicated this region in overall MG, while its early-onset replication was unsuccessful.

treatments:
- name: Pyridostigmine
  description: Acetylcholinesterase inhibition increases acetylcholine availability for symptomatic improvement without suppressing autoantibody production. MuSK-positive patients may respond poorly or tolerate it poorly; treatment is individualized.
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38152089
    reference_title: Guideline for the management of myasthenic syndromes.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: For symptomatic therapy of MG, acetylcholinesterase inhibitors (AChE-I), primarily pyridostigmine, should be used.
    explanation: Symptomatic recommendation.

    reference_title: Guideline for the management of myasthenic syndromes.

    explanation: Important serotype-specific limitation.
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE

- name: Corticosteroids
  description: Glucocorticoids are basic immunotherapy for inadequately controlled ocular or generalized MG. Use the lowest effective exposure with monitoring for infection, metabolic, bone, and other toxicity; early transient worsening and comorbidity influence initiation and bridging care.
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38152089
    reference_title: Guideline for the management of myasthenic syndromes.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Oral glucocorticosteroids (GKS) should be used to treat mild/moderate to (highly) active gMG/juvenile MG (jMG) and ocular MG (oMG) as basic immunotherapeutics at doses appropriate to disease severity over the shortest possible period of time, taking into account comorbidities, contraindications, and side effects.
    explanation: Guideline frames benefit against treatment burden.
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  treatment_term:

- name: Azathioprine
  description: A steroid-sparing immunosuppressant with delayed onset over months. Clinical and laboratory monitoring is necessary. Favorable outcomes in a late-onset observational cohort support applicability to older-onset patients but do not isolate a randomized combination effect.

    snippet: Due to the slow onset of action, therapeutic success with monotherapy is not to be expected before several months have elapsed.
    explanation: Delayed azathioprine response in the relevant guideline section.

- name: Mycophenolate Mofetil
  description: Mycophenolate is used as an alternative steroid-sparing therapy. Short randomized trials did not demonstrate the intended initial or steroid-sparing advantages; longer observational follow-up suggested benefit. It should not be presented as proven equivalent to azathioprine.

- name: Thymectomy for Nonthymomatous AChR-Positive Generalized MG
  description: MGTX randomized 126 adults aged 18–65 with AChR-positive generalized nonthymomatous MG of less than five years duration to extended transsternal thymectomy plus prednisone or prednisone alone. Surgery improved the three-year time-weighted QMG score and reduced steroid requirements. Older adults were included; age-subgroup analyses do not establish benefit exclusively in early-onset disease. Evidence for very old patients, long-standing disease, pure ocular disease, and other surgical approaches is less direct. MuSK-positive MG without a tumor is not an immunological indication.
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:27509100
    reference_title: Randomized Trial of Thymectomy in Myasthenia Gravis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Patients 18 to 65 years of age who had generalized nonthymomatous myasthenia gravis with a disease duration of less than 5 years were included
    explanation: Enrollment age is not a proof of exclusively early-onset disease.
  - reference: PMID:27509100
    reference_title: Randomized Trial of Thymectomy in Myasthenia Gravis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Patients who underwent thymectomy had a lower time-weighted average Quantitative Myasthenia Gravis score over a 3-year period than those who received prednisone alone (6.15 vs. 8.99, P<0.001)
    explanation: Randomized clinical outcome.
  - reference: PMID:40022458
    reference_title: Can Non-Thymomatous Late-Onset Myasthenia Gravis Benefit From Thymectomy? A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Thymectomy may be a potentially effective treatment for LOMG, particularly in patients who undergo the procedure soon after diagnosis. A randomized controlled study for LOMG patients is needed.
    explanation: Mostly observational meta-analysis supports continued uncertainty for a dedicated late-onset treatment effect.
  therapeutic_modality: SURGERY

    preferred_term: Thymectomy

      id: NCIT:C29894
      label: Thymectomy
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Ectopic Thymic Germinal Center Formation
    treatment_effect: INHIBITS
    description: Removes the thymic tissue harboring ectopic follicles; persistent peripheral autoreactive cells can sustain disease.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:39105625
      reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: the future is here.'
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
      directness: DIRECT
      snippet: The precise mechanism by which thymus removal imparts clinical benefit is not fully understood.
      explanation: Clinical benefit does not prove a single mediator.
    - reference: PMID:39105625
      reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: the future is here.'
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL

      directness: INDIRECT
      snippet: One likely hypothesis is that thymectomy eliminates a considerable source of antigenic stimulation, ultimately reducing the production of AChR antibodies, but autoreactive cells have exited the thymus remain and drive production of pathogenic antibodies
      explanation: Supports removal of a thymic antigenic source while qualifying the mediator and persistent peripheral activity.

- name: Plasma Exchange or Immunoadsorption
  description: Rapid removal of circulating humoral factors is used for crisis, severe exacerbation, and selected perioperative stabilization. Benefit is temporary without continuing disease control. MuSK-positive disease may respond particularly well, although comparative serotype-specific trials are limited; vascular, coagulation, and hemodynamic risks matter in older patients.

- name: Vaccination and Infection-Risk Management
  description: Review vaccination and infection risks before immunotherapy. Complement inhibition requires meningococcal precautions and, where urgent initiation precludes adequate vaccine protection, antibiotic prophylaxis according to the applicable regimen. Live vaccines are generally avoided during immunotherapy. Protection is not complete after vaccination.
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38152089
    reference_title: Guideline for the management of myasthenic syndromes.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: If the effectiveness of the vaccination cannot be waited for or if a vaccination success cannot be assumed due to the immunotherapy, prophylactic antibiotic therapy should be started in addition to the vaccination.
    explanation: Guideline complement-inhibitor precaution.
- name: Clinical and Treatment-Toxicity Monitoring
  description: Assess clinical strength, function, MG-ADL or related scales, quality of life, and treatment toxicity over time. Routine antibody titers should not substitute for clinical disease-activity assessment.

- name: Fatigue Management and Rehabilitation

  description: Arrange specialist counseling before or during pregnancy, review drug-specific fetal risks and treatment alternatives, and coordinate maternal and neonatal care. Transient neonatal disease from maternal antibody transfer is distinct from inherited congenital myasthenic syndromes.

- name: Medication and Exacerbation Education

epidemiology:
- name: Increasing late-onset incidence in a Danish population
  description: A population study of nonthymomatous AChR-seropositive MG in Denmark during 1970–1999 found stable early-onset incidence and rising late-onset incidence. These historical, serotype-specific rates are not a contemporary global incidence estimate for all adult-onset MG.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:16186537
    reference_title: Increasing incidence of late-onset anti-AChR antibody-seropositive myasthenia gravis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: PRIMARY_RESULT
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: The mean annual incidence rate of early-onset MG was constant at 3.5 x 10(-6). In late-onset MG, the rate increased from 4.7 to 20.8 x 10(-6).
    explanation: Historical population observation; not proof of a particular environmental cause.
- name: Contemporary MG prevalence context
  description: A 2025 epidemiology review summarizes approximately 20 incident cases per million person-years and 25 prevalent cases per 100,000 for MG overall. These are not measured adult-onset-only estimates. Population aging, improved ascertainment, and survival contribute to increasing prevalence.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:40675734
    reference_title: Epidemiology of myasthenia gravis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Recent high-quality studies have found an annual MG incidence of approximately 20 per million person-years, and a prevalence of approximately 25 per 100,000 population.
    explanation: All-MG context, deliberately not encoded as a prevalence band for this subtype.
  - reference: PMID:40675734
    reference_title: Epidemiology of myasthenia gravis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: MG prevalence has increased in recent years because of more elderly people in the general population, improved case finding, and improved MG treatment with reduced mortality.
    explanation: The review identifies multiple contributors rather than a single biological cause.
diagnosis:
- name: Clinical assessment with confirmatory testing
  description: Identify fatigable, fluctuating weakness and its ocular, bulbar, respiratory, axial, or limb distribution. Confirm a compatible syndrome with antibody testing and/or neuromuscular electrophysiology; exclude mimics when the pattern or treatment response is atypical.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38152089
    reference_title: Guideline for the management of myasthenic syndromes.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: The diagnosis of MG is based on the history and physical findings of fatigable and fluctuating muscle weakness. The diagnosis is confirmed by positive findings in auto-Ab diagnostics and/or electrophysiology and/or pharmacological testing.
    explanation: Guideline diagnostic framework.
- name: Serological subtyping
  description: Test AChR and, where appropriate, MuSK and other antibodies using validated assays. Cell-based AChR testing can identify some conventionally seronegative patients. LRP4 and striational antibody findings require clinical context; they do not independently establish MG.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39105625
    reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: the future is here.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Recently, cell-based assays have been developed with plasma membrane expression of the AChR, allowing the antigen to resemble the native receptor more closely
    explanation: Describes improved antigen presentation.
  - reference: PMID:39105625
    reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: the future is here.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: although they are also present in patients with motor neuron disease and patients without evidence of diseases
    explanation: Limits LRP4 specificity.
- name: Repetitive nerve stimulation and single-fiber electromyography
  description: Electrophysiology supports impaired neuromuscular transmission, especially when serology is negative. Sensitivity depends on muscles tested and phenotype. SFEMG is sensitive but nonspecific; abnormal limb jitter in ocular MG does not by itself prove clinical generalization.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:33458590
    reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: MuSK MG, late-onset MG and ocular MG.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Repetitive nerve stimulation is not very useful with purely ocular symptoms. Single fiber EMG is very sensitive, but one has to be careful remembering that it is not specific to MG
    explanation: Test-specific limitations.
  - reference: PMID:33458590
    reference_title: 'Myasthenia gravis: MuSK MG, late-onset MG and ocular MG.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Presence of abnormalities in limb muscles of a patient with OMG does not indicate generalization
    explanation: Clinical distribution cannot be inferred solely from abnormal limb jitter.
- name: Thymoma assessment
  description: Evaluate the mediastinum with chest imaging for thymoma. A negative striational-antibody test does not replace imaging.
  evidence:
  - reference: url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/books/NBK559331/
    reference_title: Myasthenia Gravis - StatPearls - NCBI Bookshelf
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: Chest computed tomography (CT) or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) should be performed in patients diagnosed with MG to assess for thymoma.
    explanation: Explicit imaging recommendation.
- name: Review of seronegative and atypical presentations
  description: Consider congenital myasthenic syndromes, other neuromuscular disorders, and structural ocular or brainstem causes when appropriate. Adult presentation does not exclude a congenital myasthenic syndrome; genetic testing addresses that differential, not routine confirmation of autoimmune MG.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38152089

    explanation: Adult differential diagnosis.
